Not prescriptive.\n\n---\n\n## Revision Log\n\n| Version | Change |\n|---------|--------|\n| v1.0 | Initial schema documentation from all source files. 12 sections, 9 gaps identified. |\n| v1.1 | Added: Section 0 (Documentation Scope), Source of Truth Hierarchy, Schema Versioning & Migration Registry, Dependency Graph (Section 6). Replaced SQL-based recommendations in all 9 gaps with architecture-level future directions. No table definitions, constraints, or gap identifications changed. |\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 0 — DOCUMENTATION SCOPE\n\n### 0.1 Purpose of This Document\n\nThis document is a **descriptive specification** of the EduX database schema as found in the source files. It was produced by reading all database definition files and documenting their state at the time of the audit.\n\n**This document does NOT:**\n- Prescribe future schema changes\n- Generate or recommend SQL scripts (no ALTER, no DROP, no RENAME)\n- Redesign any table, column, or relationship\n- Override the live production database as the authoritative state\n\n**This document DOES:**\n- Describe every table, column, constraint, FK, and index as found in source files\n- Identify gaps between the current schema and Technical Specification v1.0 (EPIC 0.8)\n- Provide architecture-level direction for resolving gaps (what must change conceptually, not how)\n- Serve as the baseline reference for all teams building on this database\n\n### 0.2 Audience\n\n| Audience | How to Use |\n|----------|-----------|\n| Backend Engineers | Reference for column names, types, and constraints during feature development |\n| Frontend Engineers | Reference for data shape and expected field types in API responses |\n| Tech Lead / Architect | Baseline for planning schema evolution in future Epics |\n| New Team Members | First-read orientation to the data model |\n\n### 0.3 Scope Boundaries\n\nThis document covers all tables created by the following files (in execution order):\n\n1. `backend/schema.sql`\n2. `backend/schemas/course_detail_schema.sql`\n3. `backend/migrate_tutor_profiles.sql`\n4. `backend/payment_migration.sql`\n5. `backend/messages_migration.sql`\n6. `backend/parent_migration.sql`\n7. `backend/assessment_migration.sql`\n8. `backend/exam_papers_migration.sql`\n9. `backend/user_management_migration.sql`\n10. `backend/parent_features_migration.sql`\n11. `backend/tutor_requests_migration.sql`\n12. `backend/schema_lesson_feedbacks.sql`\n13. `backend/server.js` (auto-migrate on startup)\n\nTables defined only in application business logic (outside these 13 layers) are out of scope.\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 1 — DATABASE OVERVIEW\n\n### 1.1 Engine & Host\n\n| Property | Value |\n|----------|-------|\n| Engine | PostgreSQL |\n| Host | Supabase |\n| Connection | Pool-based via `pool.query()` in server.js |\n| ORM | None — raw SQL |\n| Schema namespace | `public` (default) |\n\n### 1.2 Extensions in Use\n\n| Extension | Purpose | Source |\n|-----------|---------|--------|\n| `pgcrypto` | `gen_random_uuid()` for primary keys | Implicit across all tables |\n| `uuid-ossp` | `uuid_generate_v4()` | `schema_lesson_feedbacks.sql` only |\n\nNote: `lesson_feedbacks` uses `uuid_generate_v4()` (uuid-ossp) while all other tables use `gen_random_uuid()` (pgcrypto). Both produce functionally identical UUID v4 values but require different extensions to be installed.\n\n### 1.3 Global Conventions\n\n| Convention | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Primary Key type | UUID |\n| Timestamp type | `TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()` |\n| Auto-update timestamp | `update_updated_at()` trigger — applied to tables in schema.sql; NOT applied to payment tables |\n| Soft Delete | Not implemented — no `deleted_at` column anywhere |\n| Status archival pattern | Status column + `is_banned` flag |\n| Status value casing | Lowercase in DB (`pending`, `approved`) — conflicts with Technical Spec — see GAP-001 |\n| Decimal precision | `NUMERIC(15,2)` — see GAP-008 |\n\n### 1.4 Source of Truth Hierarchy\n\nWhen the database state is ambiguous or sources conflict, resolve using this hierarchy (highest authority first):\n\n```\nLEVEL 1 — AUTHORITATIVE\n  Live Production Database (Supabase)\n  → The actual running state. No document overrides what is in production.\n\nLEVEL 2 — EXECUTION HISTORY\n  Migration execution history (the order .sql files were actually applied)\n  → Determines which CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS \"won\" on conflicting definitions.\n  → Not recorded in any schema_migrations table — must be inferred from deployment logs.\n\nLEVEL 3 — SOURCE FILES\n  backend/schema.sql                (baseline schema)\n  backend/schemas/*.sql             (domain schemas)\n  backend/*_migration.sql           (incremental migrations)\n  backend/server.js auto-migrate    (runtime migrations, runs on every startup)\n\nLEVEL 4 — THIS DOCUMENT\n  EPIC 0.9 Specification v1.1\n  → Describes what the source files say.\n  → Accurate at time of writing; production state may have drifted.\n```\n\n**Implication for engineers:** If this document states a column exists but it is absent in production, production wins. File a gap update and correct this document — do not assume this document is current.\n\n### 1.5 Schema Versioning & Migration Tracking\n\n**Current Schema Version:** 1.13 (13 discrete migration layers applied in sequence)\n\nThere is currently **no `schema_migrations` table** in the database. No formal version tracking exists. The schema version above is derived by counting migration layers — it is a documentation artifact, not a database value.\n\n**Migration Registry** (execution order matters — see GAP-004):\n\n| # | File | Layer Type | Tables Affected |\n|---|------|-----------|----------------|\n| 1 | `backend/schema.sql` | Baseline schema | users, tutor_profiles, classes, class_members, materials, assignments, lessons, discussions, discussion_replies, learning_paths, learning_path_steps, student_tutor_interactions |\n| 2 | `backend/schemas/course_detail_schema.sql` | Domain schema | courses (v1 — CONFLICT), lessons (CONFLICT), assignments (CONFLICT), materials (CONFLICT), course_progress (v1) |\n| 3 | `backend/migrate_tutor_profiles.sql` | ALTER migration | tutor_profiles (+14 columns) |\n| 4 | `backend/payment_migration.sql` | Domain migration | wallets, transactions, disputes + 4 stored functions + 1 trigger |\n| 5 | `backend/messages_migration.sql` | Domain migration | messages |\n| 6 | `backend/parent_migration.sql` | Domain migration | parent_children, student_link_codes |\n| 7 | `backend/assessment_migration.sql` | Domain migration + seed data | quizzes, quiz_questions, quiz_attempts, practice_sessions |\n| 8 | `backend/exam_papers_migration.sql` | Domain migration + seed data | exam_papers, exam_paper_questions, exam_paper_attempts |\n| 9 | `backend/user_management_migration.sql` | ALTER migration | users (+is_banned) |\n| 10 | `backend/parent_features_migration.sql` | Domain migration | tutor_sessions, tutor_reviews, invoices, notifications |\n| 11 | `backend/tutor_requests_migration.sql` | Domain migration | tutor_requests |\n| 12 | `backend/schema_lesson_feedbacks.sql` | Domain migration + RLS | lesson_feedbacks |\n| 13 | `backend/server.js` (auto-migrate) | Runtime migration | login_logs, tutor_certificates, reviews, coupons, wishlists, courses (v2), course_lessons, course_enrollments, course_progress (v2) |\n\n**Compatibility Notes:**\n- Layers 1 and 2 have known conflicts (GAP-003, GAP-004). Actual table state depends on production execution order.\n- Layer 13 runs on every server.js startup (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` — idempotent). It cannot retroactively fix conflicts from earlier layers.\n- No rollback scripts exist for any layer.\n- No `schema_migrations` table exists to record which layers have been applied or when.\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 2 — ENTITY-RELATIONSHIP OVERVIEW\n\n### 2.1 Core Entity Map\n\n```\nIDENTITY DOMAIN\n  users (central hub — all domains reference this table)\n    ├── tutor_profiles        (1:1, tutor role only)\n    ├── tutor_certificates    (1:N, tutor role only)\n    ├── wallets               (1:1, all roles — created by trigger)\n    ├── login_logs            (1:N, audit)\n    └── is_banned             (direct column on users)\n\nCOMMUNICATION DOMAIN\n  messages        (N:N via sender_id / receiver_id → users)\n  notifications   (N:1 → users)\n\nPARENTAL DOMAIN\n  parent_children      (N:N → users: parent_id + student_id)\n  student_link_codes   (1:1 CHAR(8) code per student)\n\nCLASS WORKSPACE DOMAIN\n  classes               (tutor_id → users)\n    ├── class_members   (N:N → users)\n    ├── materials*      (class_id FK)\n    ├── assignments*    (class_id FK)\n    ├── lessons*        (class_id FK)\n    ├── discussions     → discussion_replies\n    └── learning_paths  → learning_path_steps\n  * subject to GAP-004 conflict\n\nCOURSE MODULE DOMAIN\n  courses            (tutor_id FK — canonical server.js version)\n  course_lessons     (course_id FK)\n  course_enrollments (user_id + course_id FK)\n  course_progress    (enrollment_id + lesson_id FK — v2 canonical)\n\nASSESSMENT DOMAIN\n  quizzes → quiz_questions → quiz_attempts\n  exam_papers → exam_paper_questions → exam_paper_attempts\n  practice_sessions (standalone)\n\nMATCHING & INTERACTION DOMAIN\n  tutor_requests          (30+ columns, 14-step wizard, student_id nullable)\n  student_tutor_interactions\n  tutor_sessions          (booking records)\n  tutor_reviews\n  lesson_feedbacks        (RLS enabled, FK constraints absent — GAP-007)\n  reviews                 (reviewer_id only, missing tutor_id — GAP-009)\n\nFINANCE DOMAIN\n  wallets        (balance + held_balance, co-owned with Identity)\n  transactions   (PostgreSQL ENUM types — GAP-005)\n  disputes       (1:1 → transactions)\n  invoices       (invoice_no UNIQUE)\n  coupons\n  wishlists      (user_id + course_id)\n```\n\n### 2.2 Central Entity: users\n\nThe `users` table is the database's hub. Every domain has at least one FK to `users`. Roles supported: `student`, `parent`, `tutor`, `admin`.\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 3 — TABLE CATALOG\n\n### 3.1 Identity & Auth Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| users | schema.sql | Central hub, 4 roles, + is_banned column |\n| tutor_profiles | schema.sql + migrate_tutor_profiles.sql | 14 columns added by migration |\n| tutor_certificates | server.js auto-migrate | Runtime creation |\n| wallets | payment_migration.sql | Created automatically by trigger |\n| login_logs | server.js auto-migrate | No FK to users — audit log |\n\n### 3.2 Communication Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| messages | messages_migration.sql | sender_id + receiver_id → users |\n| notifications | parent_features_migration.sql | user_id → users |\n\n### 3.3 Parental Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| parent_children | parent_migration.sql | N:N parent ↔ student |\n| student_link_codes | parent_migration.sql | CHAR(8) unique code per student |\n\n### 3.4 Class Workspace Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| classes | schema.sql | Tutor-created groups |\n| class_members | schema.sql | N:N classes ↔ users |\n| materials | schema.sql | class_id FK — see GAP-004 |\n| assignments | schema.sql | class_id FK — see GAP-004 |\n| lessons | schema.sql | class_id FK — see GAP-004 |\n| discussions | schema.sql | class_id FK |\n| discussion_replies | schema.sql | discussion_id FK |\n| learning_paths | schema.sql | class_id FK |\n| learning_path_steps | schema.sql | learning_path_id FK |\n\n### 3.5 Course Module Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| courses | server.js auto-migrate (v2) | tutor_id FK — canonical (see GAP-003) |\n| course_lessons | server.js auto-migrate | course_id FK |\n| course_enrollments | server.js auto-migrate | user_id + course_id FK |\n| course_progress | server.js auto-migrate (v2) | enrollment_id + lesson_id FK |\n\n### 3.6 Assessment Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| quizzes | assessment_migration.sql | Includes seed data (2 quizzes) |\n| quiz_questions | assessment_migration.sql | quiz_id FK |\n| quiz_attempts | assessment_migration.sql | quiz_id + student_id FK |\n| practice_sessions | assessment_migration.sql | student_id FK |\n| exam_papers | exam_papers_migration.sql | Includes seed data (4 papers) |\n| exam_paper_questions | exam_papers_migration.sql | exam_paper_id FK |\n| exam_paper_attempts | exam_papers_migration.sql | exam_paper_id + student_id FK |\n\n### 3.7 Matching & Interaction Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| tutor_requests | tutor_requests_migration.sql | 30+ columns, guest-capable |\n| student_tutor_interactions | schema.sql | student_id + tutor_id FK |\n| tutor_sessions | parent_features_migration.sql | Booking records |\n| tutor_reviews | parent_features_migration.sql | Review per session |\n| lesson_feedbacks | schema_lesson_feedbacks.sql | RLS enabled, FKs missing (GAP-007) |\n| reviews | server.js auto-migrate | reviewer_id SET NULL, no tutor_id (GAP-009) |\n\n### 3.8 Finance Domain\n\n| Table | Source File | Notes |\n|-------|------------|-------|\n| wallets | payment_migration.sql | held_balance for escrow |\n| transactions | payment_migration.sql | PostgreSQL ENUM types (GAP-005) |\n| disputes | payment_migration.sql | transaction_id FK |\n| invoices | parent_features_migration.sql | invoice_no UNIQUE |\n| coupons | server.js auto-migrate | Standalone discount codes |\n| wishlists | server.js auto-migrate | user_id + course_id FK |\n\n**Stored Functions (payment_migration.sql):**\n- `process_deposit(user_uuid, amount)` — adds to wallet.balance\n- `hold_money_for_lesson(student_uuid, tutor_uuid, amount)` — moves balance → held_balance\n- `release_escrow(transaction_uuid)` — releases held_balance to tutor\n- `refund_escrow(transaction_uuid)` — returns held_balance to student\n\n**Triggers:**\n- `create_wallet_for_new_user` — AFTER INSERT ON users → auto-creates wallet\n- `update_updated_at()` function — defined in schema.sql, applied to all schema.sql tables; NOT applied to payment tables\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 4 — COLUMN DEFINITIONS\n\n### 4.1 users\n\n| Column | Type 6.1 Table Ownership by Domain\n\n```\nIDENTITY DOMAIN\n  Owns:    users, tutor_profiles, tutor_certificates, wallets, login_logs\n  Note:    users is the universal anchor — no other table is independent of it\n\nCOMMUNICATION DOMAIN\n  Owns:    messages, notifications\n  Depends: users (sender_id, receiver_id, user_id)\n\nPARENTAL DOMAIN\n  Owns:    parent_children, student_link_codes\n  Depends: users (parent_id, student_id)\n\nCLASS WORKSPACE DOMAIN\n  Owns:    classes, class_members, materials*, assignments*, lessons*,\n           discussions, discussion_replies, learning_paths, learning_path_steps\n  Depends: users (tutor_id in classes, user_id in class_members)\n  *Subject to GAP-004 schema conflict\n\nCOURSE MODULE DOMAIN\n  Owns:    courses, course_lessons, course_enrollments, course_progress\n  Depends: users (tutor_id in courses, user_id in enrollments)\n  Note:    courses has unresolved conflict with course_detail_schema.sql — GAP-003\n\nASSESSMENT DOMAIN\n  Owns:    quizzes, quiz_questions, quiz_attempts,\n           practice_sessions,\n           exam_papers, exam_paper_questions, exam_paper_attempts\n  Depends: users (student_id in attempts)\n\nMATCHING & INTERACTION DOMAIN\n  Owns:    tutor_requests, student_tutor_interactions, tutor_sessions,\n           tutor_reviews, lesson_feedbacks, reviews\n  Depends: users (student_id, tutor_id across all tables)\n\nFINANCE DOMAIN\n  Owns:    wallets*, transactions, disputes, invoices, coupons, wishlists\n  Depends: users (via wallet trigger), courses (wishlists.course_id)\n  *wallets co-owned with Identity — created by identity trigger\n```\n\n### 6.2 Cross-Domain Dependency Map\n\n```\nusers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  │\n  ├── [Identity]    tutor_profiles (1:1)\n  ├── [Identity]    wallets (1:1 via trigger)\n  │                   └── [Finance] transactions (wallet_id)\n  │                                  └── [Finance] disputes\n  ├── [Class]       classes (tutor_id)\n  │                   └── class_members, materials, assignments, lessons\n  ├── [Course]      courses (tutor_id — canonical)\n  │                   ├── course_lessons, course_enrollments, course_progress\n  │                   └── [Finance] wishlists (course_id)\n  ├── [Matching]    tutor_requests (student_id, nullable)\n  ├── [Matching]    tutor_sessions (student_id + tutor_id)\n  ├── [Matching]    lesson_feedbacks (tutor_id, student_id — NO FK)\n  ├── [Matching]    reviews (reviewer_id, NO tutor_id — GAP-009)\n  ├── [Comms]       messages (sender_id + receiver_id)\n  ├── [Comms]       notifications (user_id)\n  └── [Parental]    parent_children (parent_id + student_id)\n```\n\n### 6.3 Shared Tables (Multiple Domain Consumers)\n\n| Table | Primary Owner | Secondary Consumers |\n|-------|-------------|-------------------|\n| users | Identity | All domains |\n| wallets | Identity / Finance | Finance operations |\n| courses | Course Module | Finance (wishlists), Matching (tutor profile visibility) |\n| tutor_profiles | Identity | Matching domain, Class Workspace queries |\n\n### 6.4 Orphan-Risk Tables\n\nTables where FK constraints are absent — integrity maintained at application layer only:\n\n| Table | Missing FK | Risk |\n|-------|-----------|------|\n| lesson_feedbacks | lesson_id, tutor_id, student_id | Orphaned feedback if related entities deleted |\n| reviews | tutor_id (column absent) | Cannot JOIN to tutor — rating queries broken |\n| login_logs | user_id (no FK defined) | Orphaned logs — acceptable for immutable audit |\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 7 — INDEX STRATEGY\n\n### 7.1 Index Inventory\n\n| Index Name | Table | Column | Type |\n|-----------|-------|--------|------|\n| idx_lesson_feedbacks_student_id | lesson_feedbacks | student_id | BTREE |\n| (implicit UNIQUE) | tutor_profiles | user_id | UNIQUE |\n| (implicit UNIQUE) | wallets | user_id | UNIQUE |\n| (implicit UNIQUE) | users | email | UNIQUE |\n| (implicit UNIQUE) | invoices | invoice_no | UNIQUE |\n\n### 7.2 Index Gaps Observed\n\nNo explicit indexes exist on the following high-frequency query targets:\n- `transactions.wallet_id`\n- `tutor_requests.student_id`, `tutor_requests.match_status`\n- `messages.sender_id`, `messages.receiver_id`\n- `class_members.class_id`, `class_members.user_id`\n\nThese are observations. Actual query patterns in production determine whether indexes are warranted.\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 8 — CONSTRAINTS & ENUM TYPES\n\n### 8.1 CHECK Constraints by Table\n\n| Table | Column | Allowed Values |\n|-------|--------|---------------|\n| users | role | 'student', 'parent', 'tutor', 'admin' |\n| tutor_profiles | status | 'pending', 'approved', 'rejected' |\n| tutor_requests | match_status | 'pending', 'matching', 'matched', 'closed' |\n| lesson_feedbacks | focus_rating | 1–5 (SMALLINT range) |\n| lesson_feedbacks | understanding_level | 'Tốt', 'Khá', 'Cần cố gắng' |\n| lesson_feedbacks | homework_status | 'Đã hoàn thành', 'Chưa hoàn thành', 'Không có bài tập' |\n\n### 8.2 PostgreSQL ENUM Types\n\nDefined in `payment_migration.sql` as database-level type objects:\n\n```\ntransaction_type:   DEPOSIT | WITHDRAW | PAYMENT | REFUND | COMMISSION\ntransaction_status: PENDING | SUCCESS | FAILED | HELD_IN_ESCROW | RELEASED | REFUNDED | DISPUTED\n```\n\nPostgreSQL ENUMs require `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` to extend. This cannot be rolled back. See GAP-005.\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 9 — SOFT DELETE & DATA LIFECYCLE\n\n### 9.1 Deletion Strategy\n\nSoft delete is not implemented. No `deleted_at` column exists on any table (see GAP-002).\n\nCurrent archival patterns in use:\n- `users`: `is_banned = TRUE` (record retained, access blocked)\n- `tutor_profiles`: `status = 'rejected'` (effective archival)\n- `tutor_requests`: `match_status = 'closed'`\n- Financial records: permanent retention (implicit audit requirement)\n\n### 9.2 Data Retention Gaps\n\n- `login_logs`: no TTL or cleanup mechanism documented\n- Assessment seed data embedded in migration files — not separable from schema\n- No cascade delete strategy documented for full user account deletion\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 10 — PERFORMANCE CONSIDERATIONS\n\n### 10.1 High-Volume Tables\n\n| Table | Expected Growth | Notes |\n|-------|----------------|-------|\n| transactions | High — every payment event | No partitioning strategy |\n| login_logs | High — every auth event | No TTL / cleanup mechanism |\n| messages | High — all direct messages | No archival strategy |\n| notifications | Medium-high | No read-cleanup mechanism |\n\n### 10.2 Stored Function Performance\n\nThe 4 escrow stored functions execute within single transactions. They handle atomic balance operations. No performance issues documented at current scale. Under high concurrency, `hold_money_for_lesson` may create row-level lock contention on `wallets`.\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 11 — SECURITY & ACCESS CONTROL\n\n### 11.1 Row Level Security (RLS)\n\nOnly `lesson_feedbacks` has RLS enabled via Supabase. All other tables rely on application-layer access control: JWT verification + role-check middleware in server.js.\n\n### 11.2 Sensitive Columns\n\n| Table | Column | Protection |\n|-------|--------|-----------|\n| users | password_hash | Bcrypt-hashed — never returned in API responses |\n| wallets | balance, held_balance | Application-layer permission check |\n| transactions | amount | Application-layer permission check |\n| messages | content | No encryption at rest documented |\n\n---\n\n## SECTION 12 — SPECIFICATION GAPS\n\nGaps represent divergences between the current database schema and Technical Specification v1.0 (EPIC 0.8), or internal consistency issues. Each gap is preserved exactly from v1.0. The **Future Direction** blocks in this version replace the SQL-based recommendations from v1.0 with architecture-level guidance.\n\n**Severity Classification:**\n- **P0** — Blocks a dependent Epic; must be resolved before that Epic begins\n- **P1** — Does not block current work but creates technical debt or consistency risk\n- **P2** — Minor inconsistency; can be deferred without material impact\n\n---\n\n### GAP-001 — Status Value Casing Convention\n\n**Severity:** P1\n**Affects:** All tables with status columns\n\n**Current Database State:**\nAll status CHECK constraints use lowercase values: `pending`, `approved`, `rejected`, `matching`, `matched`, `closed`.\n\n**Technical Specification v1.0 Mandate:**\nSection 4 specifies SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE for status/ENUM values: `PENDING`, `APPROVED`, `REJECTED`.\n\n**Impact:**\nAPI responses return lowercase values. If the frontend or middleware normalizes to SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE, comparison logic breaks across the stack. Inconsistency between spec and database creates onboarding confusion and subtle query bugs.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P1 — resolve before Sprint 3):**\nArchitectural concern: the status casing convention must be locked before any new status-dependent feature is built. Two paths exist: (a) amend Technical Specification v1.1 to formally accept lowercase as canonical — the simpler path since the database already uses it uniformly — or (b) define a normalization layer at the API response boundary that maps lowercase DB values to SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE API values. The chosen path must be documented in a Technical Specification update and applied consistently across all domains. Mixing conventions across tables or API endpoints is not acceptable. This decision belongs in the Tech Lead's Epic 0 review.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-002 — No Soft Delete Support\n\n**Severity:** P1\n**Affects:** All tables\n\n**Current Database State:**\nNo `deleted_at TIMESTAMPTZ` column exists on any table. Archival is status-based only.\n\n**Technical Specification v1.0 Mandate:**\nSection 4 recommends `deleted_at TIMESTAMPTZ` for recoverable deletion.\n\n**Impact:**\nHard deletes are permanent with no recovery path. No audit trail for record removal. Cascade deletes on FK relationships can silently orphan related records in tables without FK constraints.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P1 — architectural decision before Epic 1):**\nArchitectural concern: soft delete should not be applied uniformly to all tables without domain-level analysis. For the EduX business model, financial records (transactions, invoices, disputes) must never be deleted under any circumstance. Tutor profiles and user accounts benefit from soft delete for account recovery and compliance. Class content (lessons, assignments) may warrant soft delete for accidental-deletion recovery. The soft delete strategy must be defined per domain in a Technical Specification amendment before Epic 1 begins. The implementation scope should be narrow: Identity domain and Finance domain first. Assessment, Messaging, and Communication domains can defer. This document does not prescribe the column definition or migration approach — that is an Epic 1 implementation decision.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-003 — courses Table: instructor_name TEXT vs tutor_id UUID FK\n\n**Severity:** P0\n**Affects:** courses table, Course Module domain, Tutor Center Epic, Finance domain\n\n**Current Database State:**\n`backend/schemas/course_detail_schema.sql` (Layer 2) defines `courses` with `instructor_name TEXT NOT NULL` and no FK to users.\n`backend/server.js` auto-migrate (Layer 13) defines `courses` with `tutor_id UUID REFERENCES users(id)` and no instructor_name.\nDue to `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, the first definition to execute wins. If Layer 2 ran before Layer 13, production has `courses` with `instructor_name TEXT` and no `tutor_id`. If the server.js version ran first (e.g., in a fresh environment), the reverse is true. Both columns may co-exist if an undocumented ALTER was applied manually.\n\n**Impact:**\nAll course-tutor JOIN queries are broken without a proper `tutor_id` FK. The Tutor Center Epic cannot surface a tutor's courses. Finance queries for tutor revenue from courses are unbuildable without this relationship.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P0 — resolve before Tutor Center and Course Module Epics start):**\nArchitectural decision: `tutor_id UUID FK → users(id)` is the canonical relationship. `instructor_name TEXT` is a denormalized convenience field that may be retained for display fallback but cannot serve as the structural relationship anchor between courses and tutors. The production database state must be verified by the Tech Lead: confirm whether `tutor_id` exists on the courses table in production. If it does not, the data reconciliation approach — ensuring every course row has a valid `tutor_id` populated — must be defined as part of Epic 0 P0 fixes. The approach to get there (data backfill strategy, verification criteria, rollback plan) is an implementation decision outside the scope of this document.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-004 — Schema Conflict: lessons / assignments / materials Defined Twice\n\n**Severity:** P0\n**Affects:** lessons, assignments, materials tables; Class Workspace domain; Course Module domain\n\n**Current Database State:**\n`backend/schema.sql` (Layer 1) defines `lessons`, `assignments`, and `materials` with `class_id FK → classes(id)`.\n`backend/schemas/course_detail_schema.sql` (Layer 2) redefines the same 3 tables with `course_id FK → courses(id)`.\nDue to `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, Layer 2's definitions are silently skipped if Layer 1 ran first (the expected production case). The actual FK columns on these tables in production depend entirely on execution order.\n\n**Impact:**\nIf Layer 1 won (likely): `lessons`, `assignments`, `materials` are class-based. Any Course Module query expecting a `course_id` column on these tables will fail. The `course_detail_schema.sql` versions have effectively never existed in production.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P0 — confirm production state before any content feature development):**\nArchitectural decision: the two naming conventions serve different and non-overlapping purposes. They should not be conflated:\n1. **Class-based content** (Layer 1, canonical): `lessons(class_id)`, `assignments(class_id)`, `materials(class_id)` — these are Class Workspace Domain tables and should remain as-is.\n2. **Course-based content** (Layer 13, canonical): `course_lessons(course_id)` — this is the Course Module Domain's content table, already correctly defined in server.js auto-migrate.\n\n`course_detail_schema.sql` should be treated as a deprecated and superseded file. Its `lessons/assignments/materials` definitions with `course_id` are not needed because `course_lessons` in server.js serves that purpose. The Tech Lead must confirm this interpretation against actual production state before any Course Module or Class Workspace feature work begins. No SQL action is prescribed by this document.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-005 — transactions Table Uses PostgreSQL ENUM Types\n\n**Severity:** P1\n**Affects:** transactions table, Finance domain\n\n**Current Database State:**\n`transaction_type` and `transaction_status` are PostgreSQL ENUM type objects defined at the database level, not TEXT + CHECK constraints.\n\n**Technical Specification v1.0 Preference:**\nTEXT + CHECK constraints are preferred for operational flexibility.\n\n**Impact:**\nAdding a new transaction type or status value requires `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` in PostgreSQL. This operation cannot be executed inside a transaction block in older PostgreSQL versions, cannot be easily rolled back, and requires careful coordination in production.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P1 — confirm value completeness before Finance Epic begins):**\nArchitectural concern: the current ENUM value sets may not cover all Finance domain transaction flows. Before the Finance Center Epic begins, the Tech Lead must audit whether the current values (`DEPOSIT`, `WITHDRAW`, `PAYMENT`, `REFUND`, `COMMISSION` / `PENDING`, `SUCCESS`, `FAILED`, `HELD_IN_ESCROW`, `RELEASED`, `REFUNDED`, `DISPUTED`) cover all planned business scenarios (e.g., `CHARGEBACK`, `PARTIAL_REFUND`, `COMMISSION_REVERSAL`). If any values are anticipated to be added, the type strategy must be reconsidered at the start of the Finance Epic — not mid-sprint when it creates deployment risk. If the current values are sufficient, no action is required and this remains a P2 observation.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-006 — wallets Table Missing created_at Column\n\n**Severity:** P2\n**Affects:** wallets table\n\n**Current Database State:**\n`wallets` has `updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ` but no `created_at` column. The `create_wallet_for_new_user` trigger fires on user creation, so wallet creation time is effectively equivalent to user creation time.\n\n**Technical Specification v1.0 Mandate:**\nSection 4 requires `created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()` on all tables.\n\n**Impact:**\nCannot directly determine when a wallet was created from the wallets table. Minor audit gap — derivable via JOIN to users.created_at.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P2 — opportunistic, no Epic dependency):**\nArchitectural note: `created_at` on wallets is semantically equivalent to `users.created_at` for the same user ID, since wallets are always created by trigger at the moment of user registration. Adding `created_at` to wallets is a non-breaking additive change with no data migration complexity — the column can be back-populated from `users.created_at` via a JOIN. This can be addressed as a housekeeping task within any Epic that touches the Finance domain, without blocking any other feature.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-007 — lesson_feedbacks Missing FK Constraints\n\n**Severity:** P1\n**Affects:** lesson_feedbacks table, data integrity for the Booking domain\n\n**Current Database State:**\n`lesson_feedbacks.lesson_id`, `.tutor_id`, and `.student_id` are declared `UUID NOT NULL` but carry no FK constraints to any other table. The RLS policies correctly reference `tutor_id` and `student_id` for access control, but there is no referential integrity enforcement at the database level.\n\n**Impact:**\nOrphaned feedback records can exist if the referenced user or lesson is deleted. The `lesson_id` column has no clear target table (lessons table is class-based; no bookings table yet exists). JOIN queries to reconstruct a lesson's feedback history require application-layer assumptions about what `lesson_id` references.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P1 — resolve as part of Booking Center domain definition):**\nArchitectural concern: the `lesson_id` FK cannot be correctly defined until the Booking Center domain table is established. The question \"what does `lesson_id` reference?\" has three possible answers: `lessons(id)` (class-based session), `tutor_sessions(id)` (current booking record), or a future canonical `bookings(id)` table. This ambiguity must be resolved when the Booking Center domain is designed. At that point, `lesson_feedbacks.lesson_id` should be migrated to reference the canonical booking entity. The `tutor_id` and `student_id` FKs to `users(id)` are unambiguous and can be addressed earlier — their addition is a non-breaking constraint on existing NOT NULL UUID columns. This is an Epic 5 (Booking Center) concern for `lesson_id`, and an Epic 2 (Tutor Center) housekeeping item for `tutor_id` and `student_id`.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-008 — Amount Precision: NUMERIC(15,2) vs NUMERIC(15,0)\n\n**Severity:** P2\n**Affects:** wallets, transactions, tutor_requests (budget_min, budget_max)\n\n**Current Database State:**\nAll monetary columns use `NUMERIC(15,2)` — supports two decimal places (e.g., 150,000.50 VND).\n\n**Technical Specification v1.0:**\nSection 4 specifies `NUMERIC(15,0)` — integer-only (no decimal sub-units).\n\n**Impact:**\nIn the Vietnamese market, VND does not use decimal sub-units in practice. `NUMERIC(15,2)` is technically more flexible but inconsistent with the spec. Display formatting at the application layer must account for potential `.00` values.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P2 — correct the spec, not the database):**\nArchitectural recommendation: the Technical Specification v1.0 should be updated to reflect `NUMERIC(15,2)` as the canonical precision. Per the Source of Truth Hierarchy in this document (Section 1.4), the production database definition takes precedence over the specification when they conflict without a deliberate reason to change. There is no business reason to reduce decimal precision on monetary columns — doing so would be a breaking change with no benefit. Application-layer formatting should render VND amounts as integers (strip the `.00`) regardless of storage precision. This is a spec correction action, not a database action.\n\n---\n\n### GAP-009 — reviews Table Missing tutor_id FK\n\n**Severity:** P0\n**Affects:** reviews table, Tutor Center rating display, AI Matching Algorithm\n\n**Current Database State:**\nThe `reviews` table (Layer 13, server.js auto-migrate) has `reviewer_id UUID REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`. No `tutor_id` column exists. There is no way to JOIN the reviews table to a specific tutor from the database side.\n\n**Observed Production Consequence (P0-1 bug):**\n`POST /api/tutors/matches` hardcodes `0 AS rating, 0 AS review_count` in the SELECT clause because the reviews table cannot be queried by tutor. Every tutor in AI matching results displays 0 stars and 0 reviews.\n\n**Secondary concern:**\n`reviewer_id ON DELETE SET NULL` destroys the audit trail when a reviewer's account is deleted. A review rating should survive reviewer deletion — only the reviewer identity should be anonymized.\n\n**Future Direction (Priority: P0 — must resolve before Tutor Center Epic begins):**\nArchitectural concern: the `reviews` table is currently non-functional for EduX's core tutor reputation use case. Two structural changes are required at the architecture level:\n\n1. **Tutor relationship:** A `tutor_id UUID FK → users(id)` column must be added to reviews, linking each review to the tutor being reviewed. This is the primary key relationship that enables all rating queries, profile displays, and matching score calculations.\n\n2. **Reviewer deletion policy:** `ON DELETE SET NULL` on `reviewer_id` destroys audit trail. The correct behavior is either (a) retain the review record with `reviewer_id` nullified (current behavior — acceptable for anonymization) but ensure the rating value is preserved, or (b) use soft delete at the application layer. The data model choice must be confirmed by the Tech Lead.\n\nThe implementation approach for establishing `tutor_id` on existing `reviews` rows (if any exist) is an Epic 0 P0 fixes decision. Until this gap is closed, all tutor rating display in matching, search, profile, and analytics features is non-functional and must be treated as a known broken state.\n\n---\n\n## STATUS\n\n```\nEPIC 0.9 — Database Schema & Data Architecture Specification\nVersion:    1.1 (LOCKED)\nStatus:     Production-Grade Specification — Descriptive, not prescriptive\nMigration layers documented: 13\nTables documented: 40+\nSpecification gaps identified: 9 (P0: 3, P1: 4, P2: 2)\nP0 gaps must be resolved in Epic 0 before any domain Epic can begin work\non their affected tables\n```"
    }
  ],
